````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/controllers/order-controller.ts
import type { FastifyReply, FastifyRequest } from "fastify";
import {
  canAccessOrder,
  canActForCustomer,
  FORBIDDEN,
} from "../../../application/auth/access-policy.js";
import type { CancelOrder } from "../../../application/order/cancel-order/cancel-order.types.js";
import type { CreateOrder } from "../../../application/order/create-order/create-order.types.js";
import type { GetOrder } from "../../../application/order/get-order/get-order.types.js";
import type { ProcessPayment } from "../../../application/payment/process-payment/process-payment.types.js";
import { principalOf } from "../auth-guards.js";
import { sendApplicationError } from "../http-errors.js";
import { toOrderResponse } from "../presenters/order-presenter.js";
import { toPaymentResponse } from "../presenters/payment-presenter.js";
import {
  createOrderBodySchema,
  orderIdParamsSchema,
} from "../schemas/order-schemas.js";

type OrderControllerDependencies = {
  createOrder: CreateOrder;
  getOrder: GetOrder;
  cancelOrder: CancelOrder;
  processPayment: ProcessPayment;
};

export function createOrderController({
  createOrder,
  getOrder,
  cancelOrder,
  processPayment,
}: OrderControllerDependencies) {
  return {
    async create(request: FastifyRequest, reply: FastifyReply): Promise<void> {
      const body = createOrderBodySchema.safeParse(request.body);

      if (!body.success) {
        reply.code(400).send({ message: "Invalid request body" });
        return;
      }

      const principal = principalOf(request);

      // Ordering on someone else's behalf is an administrator action.
      if (!canActForCustomer(principal, body.data.customerId)) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, FORBIDDEN);
        return;
      }

      const result = await createOrder({ customerId: body.data.customerId });

      if (!result.ok) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, result.error);
        return;
      }

      reply.code(201).send(toOrderResponse(result.value));
    },

    async getById(request: FastifyRequest, reply: FastifyReply): Promise<void> {
      const params = orderIdParamsSchema.safeParse(request.params);

      if (!params.success) {
        reply.code(400).send({ message: "Invalid order id" });
        return;
      }

      const result = await getOrder({ id: params.data.id });

      if (!result.ok) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, result.error);
        return;
      }

      const principal = principalOf(request);

      if (!canAccessOrder(principal, result.value)) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, FORBIDDEN);
        return;
      }

      reply.code(200).send(toOrderResponse(result.value));
    },

    async cancel(request: FastifyRequest, reply: FastifyReply): Promise<void> {
      const params = orderIdParamsSchema.safeParse(request.params);

      if (!params.success) {
        reply.code(400).send({ message: "Invalid order id" });
        return;
      }

      const existing = await getOrder({ id: params.data.id });

      if (!existing.ok) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, existing.error);
        return;
      }

      const principal = principalOf(request);

      if (!canAccessOrder(principal, existing.value)) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, FORBIDDEN);
        return;
      }

      const result = await cancelOrder({ orderId: params.data.id });

      if (!result.ok) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, result.error);
        return;
      }

      reply.code(200).send(toOrderResponse(result.value));
    },

    async pay(request: FastifyRequest, reply: FastifyReply): Promise<void> {
      const params = orderIdParamsSchema.safeParse(request.params);

      if (!params.success) {
        reply.code(400).send({ message: "Invalid order id" });
        return;
      }

      const existing = await getOrder({ id: params.data.id });

      if (!existing.ok) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, existing.error);
        return;
      }

      const principal = principalOf(request);

      if (!canAccessOrder(principal, existing.value)) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, FORBIDDEN);
        return;
      }

      const result = await processPayment({ orderId: params.data.id });

      if (!result.ok) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, result.error);
        return;
      }

      reply.code(200).send({
        order: toOrderResponse(result.value.order),
        payment: toPaymentResponse(result.value.payment),
      });
    },
  };
}

export type OrderController = ReturnType<typeof createOrderController>;

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/controllers/product-controller.ts
import type { FastifyReply, FastifyRequest } from "fastify";
import type { CreateProduct } from "../../../application/product/create-product/create-product.types.js";
import type { GetProduct } from "../../../application/product/get-product/get-product.types.js";
import type { ListProducts } from "../../../application/product/list-products/list-products.types.js";
import { sendApplicationError } from "../http-errors.js";
import { toProductResponse } from "../presenters/product-presenter.js";
import {
  createProductBodySchema,
  productIdParamsSchema,
} from "../schemas/product-schemas.js";

type ProductControllerDependencies = {
  createProduct: CreateProduct;
  getProduct: GetProduct;
  listProducts: ListProducts;
};

export function createProductController({
  createProduct,
  getProduct,
  listProducts,
}: ProductControllerDependencies) {
  return {
    async create(request: FastifyRequest, reply: FastifyReply): Promise<void> {
      const body = createProductBodySchema.safeParse(request.body);

      if (!body.success) {
        reply.code(400).send({ message: "Invalid request body" });
        return;
      }

      const result = await createProduct({
        name: body.data.name,
        amountInMinorUnits: body.data.price.amountInMinorUnits,
        currency: body.data.price.currency,
        ...(body.data.availableQuantity === undefined
          ? {}
          : { availableQuantity: body.data.availableQuantity }),
      });

      if (!result.ok) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, result.error);
        return;
      }

      reply.code(201).send(toProductResponse(result.value));
    },

    async list(_request: FastifyRequest, reply: FastifyReply): Promise<void> {
      const result = await listProducts();

      if (!result.ok) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, result.error);
        return;
      }

      reply.code(200).send(result.value.map(toProductResponse));
    },

    async getById(request: FastifyRequest, reply: FastifyReply): Promise<void> {
      const params = productIdParamsSchema.safeParse(request.params);

      if (!params.success) {
        reply.code(400).send({ message: "Invalid product id" });
        return;
      }

      const result = await getProduct({ id: params.data.id });

      if (!result.ok) {
        sendApplicationError(reply, result.error);
        return;
      }

      reply.code(200).send(toProductResponse(result.value));
    },
  };
}

export type ProductController = ReturnType<typeof createProductController>;

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/fastify.ts
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import Fastify, { type FastifyError, type FastifyInstance } from "fastify";
import type { RateLimiter } from "../../application/ports/rate-limiter.js";
import type { TokenService } from "../../application/ports/token-service.js";
import type { HealthCheck } from "../health/health-check.js";
import type { Metrics } from "../observability/metrics.js";
import { registerAuthentication } from "./auth-guards.js";
import type { AuthController } from "./controllers/auth-controller.js";
import type { CartController } from "./controllers/cart-controller.js";
import type { CheckoutController } from "./controllers/checkout-controller.js";
import type { CustomerController } from "./controllers/customer-controller.js";
import type { OrderController } from "./controllers/order-controller.js";
import type { ProductController } from "./controllers/product-controller.js";
import { registerHealthRoutes } from "./health-routes.js";
import { registerRateLimit } from "./rate-limit.js";
import { registerAuthRoutes } from "./routes/auth-routes.js";
import { registerCartRoutes } from "./routes/cart-routes.js";
import { registerCheckoutRoutes } from "./routes/checkout-routes.js";
import { registerCustomerRoutes } from "./routes/customer-routes.js";
import { registerOrderRoutes } from "./routes/order-routes.js";
import { registerProductRoutes } from "./routes/product-routes.js";

export type HttpControllers = {
  auth: AuthController;
  customers: CustomerController;
  products: ProductController;
  carts: CartController;
  orders: OrderController;
  checkout: CheckoutController;
};

type HttpAppOptions = {
  controllers: HttpControllers;
  /** Verifies the bearer token that guards protected routes. */
  tokens: TokenService;
  logger?: boolean;
  /** Omitted in tests that do not care about limiting. */
  rateLimiter?: RateLimiter;
  /** Tighter budget for register/login; falls back to unlimited in tests. */
  authRateLimiter?: RateLimiter;
  metrics?: Metrics;
  healthChecks?: HealthCheck[];
};

/**
 * Builds the HTTP adapter. It never listens, so tests can drive the real
 * routes and error handler through `inject` without a socket.
 *
 * Request ids are honoured from `x-request-id` when the caller supplies one,
 * which is what makes logs and traces joinable across services.
 */
export function buildHttpApp({
  controllers,
  tokens,
  logger = true,
  rateLimiter,
  authRateLimiter,
  metrics,
  healthChecks = [],
}: HttpAppOptions): FastifyInstance {
  const app = Fastify({
    logger,
    genReqId: (request) => {
      const incoming = request.headers["x-request-id"];

      return typeof incoming === "string" &&
        incoming.length > 0 &&
        incoming.length <= 128
        ? incoming
        : randomUUID();
    },
  });

  if (metrics !== undefined) {
    app.addHook("onResponse", async (request, reply) => {
      const route =
        (request as { routeOptions?: { url?: string } }).routeOptions?.url ??
        request.url;

      metrics.observeHttpRequest({
        method: request.method,
        route,
        statusCode: reply.statusCode,
        durationSeconds: reply.elapsedTime / 1000,
      });
    });
  }

  // Cheap, dependency-free defaults. Anything that would need a policy
  // decision per route (CSP, CORS) is left to the deployment edge.
  app.addHook("onSend", async (request, reply, payload) => {
    reply.header("x-request-id", request.id);
    reply.header("x-content-type-options", "nosniff");
    reply.header("x-frame-options", "DENY");
    reply.header("referrer-policy", "no-referrer");

    // Token and profile responses must never be stored by a shared cache.
    if (request.url.startsWith("/auth/")) {
      reply.header("cache-control", "no-store");
    }

    return payload;
  });

  // Authentication is extracted once for every route; the guards decide who
  // may continue, and public routes simply ignore a missing principal.
  registerAuthentication(app, tokens);

  registerHealthRoutes(app, {
    checks: healthChecks,
    ...(metrics === undefined ? {} : { metrics }),
  });

  registerAuthRoutes(app, controllers.auth, authRateLimiter);
  registerCustomerRoutes(app, controllers.customers);
  registerProductRoutes(app, controllers.products);
  registerCartRoutes(app, controllers.carts);
  registerOrderRoutes(app, controllers.orders);
  registerCheckoutRoutes(app, controllers.checkout);

  if (rateLimiter !== undefined) {
    registerRateLimit(app, rateLimiter);
  }

  // Unexpected failures are logged once, here, and answered without detail.
  app.setErrorHandler((error, request, reply) => {
    const fastifyError = error as FastifyError;
    const statusCode = fastifyError.statusCode ?? 500;

    if (statusCode >= 500) {
      request.log.error({ err: fastifyError }, "Unhandled request error");
      return reply.code(500).send({ message: "Internal server error" });
    }

    return reply.code(statusCode).send({ message: fastifyError.message });
  });

  return app;
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/health-routes.ts
import type { FastifyInstance } from "fastify";
import type { Metrics } from "../observability/metrics.js";
import { runHealthChecks, type HealthCheck } from "../health/health-check.js";

export type HealthRoutesOptions = {
  checks: HealthCheck[];
  metrics?: Metrics;
};

/**
 * Liveness, readiness, and metrics — the three endpoints an orchestrator and a
 * scraper expect, and the only routes exempt from rate limiting.
 */
export function registerHealthRoutes(
  app: FastifyInstance,
  { checks, metrics }: HealthRoutesOptions,
): void {
  app.get("/health", async () => ({
    status: "ok",
    uptimeSeconds: Math.round(process.uptime()),
  }));

  app.get("/ready", async (_request, reply) => {
    const report = await runHealthChecks(checks);

    reply.code(report.status === "ok" ? 200 : 503);

    return report;
  });

  if (metrics !== undefined) {
    app.get("/metrics", async (_request, reply) => {
      reply.type(metrics.contentType);
      return metrics.render();
    });
  }
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/http-errors.ts
import type { FastifyReply } from "fastify";

/**
 * Error type to status. The suffix conventions below already cover the bulk of
 * the mapping, so this table only holds the types that need a specific code.
 */
const EXPLICIT_STATUSES: Record<string, number> = {
  invalid_credentials: 401,
  forbidden: 403,
  invalid_customer: 400,
  invalid_product: 400,
  invalid_inventory: 400,
  invalid_order: 400,
  invalid_payment: 400,
  invalid_cart_quantity: 400,
  cart_currency_mismatch: 409,
  insufficient_stock: 409,
  email_already_registered: 409,
  order_already_paid: 409,
  order_cancelled: 409,
  empty_cart: 409,
  item_not_in_cart: 409,
  payment_declined: 402,
  payment_gateway_unavailable: 502,
  idempotency_unavailable: 503,
  checkout_in_progress: 409,
};

export function statusForError(errorType: string): number {
  // The table wins over the suffix conventions: `invalid_credentials` is an
  // authentication failure (401), not a malformed request (400).
  const explicit = EXPLICIT_STATUSES[errorType];

  if (explicit !== undefined) {
    return explicit;
  }

  if (errorType.endsWith("_not_found")) {
    return 404;
  }

  if (errorType.startsWith("invalid_")) {
    return 400;
  }

  return 500;
}

/**
 * Expected failures explain themselves; server faults stay opaque so storage
 * or broker details never reach a client.
 */
export function sendApplicationError(
  reply: FastifyReply,
  error: { type: string; message: string },
): void {
  const status = statusForError(error.type);

  if (status >= 500) {
    reply.code(status).send({ message: "Internal server error" });
    return;
  }

  reply.code(status).send({ message: error.message });
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/presenters/auth-presenter.ts
import type { AuthenticatedSession } from "../../../application/auth/session.js";
import {
  toCustomerResponse,
  type CustomerResponse,
} from "./customer-presenter.js";

export type AuthResponse = {
  customer: CustomerResponse;
  role: string;
  accessToken: {
    token: string;
    tokenType: "Bearer";
    expiresAt: string;
  };
};

/** The credential record itself never leaves the process. */
export function toAuthResponse({
  customer,
  role,
  accessToken,
}: AuthenticatedSession): AuthResponse {
  return {
    customer: toCustomerResponse(customer),
    role,
    accessToken: {
      token: accessToken.token,
      tokenType: "Bearer",
      expiresAt: accessToken.expiresAt,
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/presenters/cart-presenter.ts
import {
  cartItemCount,
  cartTotal,
  type Cart,
} from "../../../domain/cart/cart.js";

export type CartItemResponse = {
  productId: string;
  quantity: number;
  unitPrice: {
    amountInMinorUnits: number;
    currency: string;
  };
};

export type CartResponse = {
  id: string;
  customerId: string;
  items: CartItemResponse[];
  itemCount: number;
  total: {
    amountInMinorUnits: number;
    currency: string;
  } | null;
  updatedAt: string;
};

/**
 * The cart response includes derived facts so clients never do arithmetic.
 * Deriving them with the domain functions keeps the definition of a cart total
 * in one place.
 */
export function toCartResponse(cart: Cart): CartResponse {
  return {
    id: cart.id,
    customerId: cart.customerId,
    items: cart.items.map((item) => ({
      productId: item.productId,
      quantity: item.quantity,
      unitPrice: {
        amountInMinorUnits: item.unitPrice.amountInMinorUnits,
        currency: item.unitPrice.currency,
      },
    })),
    itemCount: cartItemCount(cart),
    total: cartTotal(cart),
    updatedAt: cart.updatedAt,
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/rate-limit.ts
import type { FastifyInstance, preHandlerHookHandler } from "fastify";
import type { RateLimiter } from "../../application/ports/rate-limiter.js";

const EXEMPT_PATHS = new Set(["/health", "/ready", "/metrics"]);

/**
 * A preHandler hook, not a business rule: it runs before validation and never
 * touches a use case. The limiter itself is a port, so this works the same
 * with Redis (shared across instances) or the in-memory fallback.
 *
 * If the limiter fails (Redis outage) the request is allowed through: refusing
 * all traffic because the counter store is down is worse than under-limiting.
 */
export function createAuthRateLimitHook(
  limiter: RateLimiter,
): preHandlerHookHandler {
  /**
   * Credential endpoints carry a much smaller budget than the API default, so
   * password guessing is expensive even from a single address. The key is
   * namespaced, so this counter cannot collide with the general limiter.
   */
  return async (request, reply) => {
    const route =
      (request as { routeOptions?: { url?: string } }).routeOptions?.url ??
      request.url;

    try {
      const decision = await limiter.consume(`auth:${request.ip}:${route}`);

      if (!decision.allowed) {
        request.log.warn({ route, ip: request.ip }, "Auth rate limit exceeded");

        reply
          .header("retry-after", decision.resetInSeconds)
          .code(429)
          .send({ message: "Too many authentication attempts" });
      }
    } catch (error) {
      // Same policy as the general limiter: an unavailable counter store must
      // not lock every user out of the API.
      request.log.error(
        { err: error },
        "Auth rate limiter unavailable; allowing",
      );
    }
  };
}

export function registerRateLimit(
  app: FastifyInstance,
  limiter: RateLimiter,
): void {
  app.addHook("preHandler", async (request, reply) => {
    const path = request.url.split("?")[0] ?? request.url;

    if (EXEMPT_PATHS.has(path)) {
      return;
    }

    const route =
      (request as { routeOptions?: { url?: string } }).routeOptions?.url ??
      path;

    try {
      const decision = await limiter.consume(`${request.ip}:${route}`);

      reply
        .header("x-ratelimit-limit", decision.limit)
        .header("x-ratelimit-remaining", decision.remaining)
        .header("x-ratelimit-reset", decision.resetInSeconds);

      if (!decision.allowed) {
        request.log.warn({ route, ip: request.ip }, "Rate limit exceeded");

        return reply
          .header("retry-after", decision.resetInSeconds)
          .code(429)
          .send({ message: "Too many requests" });
      }
    } catch (error) {
      request.log.error({ err: error }, "Rate limiter unavailable; allowing");
    }
  });
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/routes/auth-routes.ts
import type { FastifyInstance } from "fastify";
import type { RateLimiter } from "../../../application/ports/rate-limiter.js";
import { requireAuth } from "../auth-guards.js";
import type { AuthController } from "../controllers/auth-controller.js";
import { createAuthRateLimitHook } from "../rate-limit.js";

/**
 * Authentication endpoints get a tighter budget than the rest of the API, and
 * `/auth/me` proves the token round trip. No route here ever returns a hash.
 */
export function registerAuthRoutes(
  app: FastifyInstance,
  controller: AuthController,
  rateLimiter?: RateLimiter,
): void {
  const authOptions =
    rateLimiter === undefined
      ? {}
      : { preHandler: createAuthRateLimitHook(rateLimiter) };

  app.post("/auth/register", authOptions, controller.register);
  app.post("/auth/login", authOptions, controller.login);
  app.get("/auth/me", { preHandler: requireAuth }, controller.me);
}

````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/routes/cart-routes.ts
import type { FastifyInstance } from "fastify";

  controller: CartController,

  app.get("/customers/:customerId/cart", { preHandler }, controller.get);
  app.post(
    "/customers/:customerId/cart/items",

    "/customers/:customerId/cart/items/:productId",
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/routes/product-routes.ts
import type { FastifyInstance } from "fastify";
import { requireAdmin, requireAuth } from "../auth-guards.js";
import type { ProductController } from "../controllers/product-controller.js";

/**
 * The catalogue is public; writing to it is an administrative action. The role
 * check is a route guard here because it depends on nothing but the token.
 */
export function registerProductRoutes(
  app: FastifyInstance,
  controller: ProductController,
): void {
  app.post(
    "/products",
    { preHandler: [requireAuth, requireAdmin] },
    controller.create,
  );
  app.get("/products", controller.list);
  app.get("/products/:id", controller.getById);
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/http/schemas/auth-schemas.ts
import { z } from "zod";

/**
 * The edge validates shape, not policy: password length is a domain rule, so
 * its failure keeps a specific message ("at least 12 characters") instead of a
 * generic "invalid request body". The upper bound only limits work done on
 * absurd payloads.
 */
export const registerBodySchema = z
  .object({
    email: z.string().min(3).max(320),
    name: z.string().min(1).max(120),
    password: z.string().min(1).max(1_024),
  })
  .strict();

export const loginBodySchema = z
  .object({
    email: z.string().min(3).max(320),
    password: z.string().min(1).max(1_024),
  })
  .strict();
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/idempotency/in-memory-idempotency-store.ts
import type {
  IdempotencyRecord,
  IdempotencyReservation,
  IdempotencyStore,
} from "../../application/ports/idempotency-store.js";

type Entry = {
  record: IdempotencyRecord<unknown>;
  expiresAt: number;
};

type Claim = {
  expiresAt: number;
};

/**
 * Disposable idempotency storage. The API mirrors the Redis adapter that will
 * replace it: lookup by key, save with a TTL, an atomic claim, lazy expiry.
 *
 * "Atomic" here is honest for one Node process because `acquire` checks and
 * writes without yielding; Redis gets the same guarantee from `SET NX`.
 */
export function createInMemoryIdempotencyStore(
  now: () => number = Date.now,
): IdempotencyStore {
  const entries = new Map<string, Entry>();
  const claims = new Map<string, Claim>();

  return {
    async find<TResult>(key: string) {
      const entry = entries.get(key);

      if (entry === undefined) {
        return null;
      }

      if (entry.expiresAt <= now()) {
        entries.delete(key);
        return null;
      }

      return structuredClone(entry.record) as IdempotencyRecord<TResult>;
    },

    async save<TResult>(
      key: string,
      record: IdempotencyRecord<TResult>,
      ttlSeconds: number,
    ) {
      entries.set(key, {
        record: structuredClone(record),
        expiresAt: now() + ttlSeconds * 1000,
      });
      claims.delete(key);
    },

    async acquire(
      key: string,
      ttlSeconds: number,
    ): Promise<IdempotencyReservation> {
      const current = claims.get(key);
      const timestamp = now();

      if (current !== undefined && current.expiresAt > timestamp) {
        return "busy";
      }

      claims.set(key, { expiresAt: timestamp + ttlSeconds * 1000 });
      return "acquired";
    },

    async release(key: string) {
      claims.delete(key);
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/inbox/in-memory-inbox-store.ts
import type { InboxStore } from "../../application/ports/inbox-store.js";

/** Per-process inbox: enough for unit tests and local runs. */
export function createInMemoryInboxStore(): InboxStore {
  const processed = new Set<string>();

  return {
    async markProcessed({ id }) {
      if (processed.has(id)) {
        return false;
      }

      processed.add(id);
      return true;
    },

    async hasProcessed(id) {
      return processed.has(id);
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/kafka/kafka-event-publisher.ts
import { Kafka, Partitioners, type Producer } from "kafkajs";
import type { EventPublisher } from "../../application/ports/event-publisher.js";
import { toKafkaMessage } from "./kafka-messages.js";

export type KafkaEventPublisherOptions = {
  brokers: string[];
  clientId: string;
  /** Producer acks. `all` waits for the in-sync replicas. */
  acks?: number;
};

export type KafkaEventPublisher = EventPublisher & {
  connect(): Promise<void>;
  disconnect(): Promise<void>;
};

/**
 * Kafka is one possible *reader* of the outbox. The dispatcher still decides
 * when an event is delivered; this adapter only decides how it travels:
 * topic, partition key, JSON body, headers.
 *
 * The producer connects lazily on the first publish, so a broker that is down
 * fails the delivery (and the outbox retries it) instead of the whole process.
 */
export function createKafkaEventPublisher({
  brokers,
  clientId,
  acks = -1,
}: KafkaEventPublisherOptions): KafkaEventPublisher {
  const kafka = new Kafka({
    clientId,
    brokers,
    retry: { retries: 5, initialRetryTime: 200 },
  });

  const producer: Producer = kafka.producer({
    allowAutoTopicCreation: true,
    createPartitioner: Partitioners.DefaultPartitioner,
  });
  let connection: Promise<void> | null = null;

  const connect = async (): Promise<void> => {
    if (connection === null) {
      connection = producer.connect().catch((error: unknown) => {
        // A failed connect must not be cached as success.
        connection = null;
        throw error;
      });
    }

    await connection;
  };

  return {
    async publish(event, envelope) {
      await connect();
      const message = toKafkaMessage(event, envelope);

      await producer.send({
        topic: message.topic,
        acks,
        messages: [
          {
            ...(message.key === null ? {} : { key: message.key }),
            value: message.value,
            headers: message.headers,
          },
        ],
      });
    },

    connect,

    async disconnect() {
      if (connection !== null) {
        await producer.disconnect();
        connection = null;
      }
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/kafka/kafka-messages.ts
import type { DomainEvent } from "../../domain/shared/domain-event.js";

/**
 * Topic names and partition keys belong to the broker world. Keeping the
 * mapping in one pure module means the application never learns a topic name,
 * and the routing is testable without a broker.
 */
export const ORDER_TOPIC = "ecommerce.orders.v1";
export const CUSTOMER_TOPIC = "ecommerce.customers.v1";

export const DEFAULT_TOPIC = "ecommerce.events.v1";

const TOPICS: Record<string, string> = {
  "customer.registered": CUSTOMER_TOPIC,
  "order.created": ORDER_TOPIC,
  "order.paid": ORDER_TOPIC,
  "order.cancelled": ORDER_TOPIC,
};

/** Declared once, created at start-up, so a consumer never races a topic. */
export const KAFKA_TOPICS = [ORDER_TOPIC, CUSTOMER_TOPIC, DEFAULT_TOPIC];

export type KafkaMessage = {
  topic: string;
  key: string | null;
  value: string;
  headers: Record<string, string>;
};

export function topicForEvent(name: string): string {
  return TOPICS[name] ?? DEFAULT_TOPIC;
}

/**
 * Orders are partitioned by order id, so all events for one order land on the
 * same partition and reach a consumer in order. Customer events fall back to
 * the customer id for the same reason.
 */
export function partitionKeyForEvent(event: DomainEvent): string | null {
  const candidate = event.payload["orderId"] ?? event.payload["customerId"];

  return typeof candidate === "string" ? candidate : null;
}

export function toKafkaMessage(
  event: DomainEvent,
  envelope: { id: string },
): KafkaMessage {
  return {
    topic: topicForEvent(event.name),
    key: partitionKeyForEvent(event),
    value: JSON.stringify({
      id: envelope.id,
      name: event.name,
      occurredAt: event.occurredAt,
      payload: event.payload,
    }),
    headers: {
      "x-event-id": envelope.id,
      "x-event-name": event.name,
      "x-event-version": "1",
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/kafka/kafka-topics.ts
import { Kafka } from "kafkajs";
import { KAFKA_TOPICS } from "./kafka-messages.js";

export type EnsureKafkaTopicsOptions = {
  brokers: string[];
  clientId: string;
  topics?: string[];
  numPartitions?: number;
};

/**
 * Topic creation belongs to the broker side of the world and belongs at
 * start-up, before any consumer subscribes. Relying on auto-creation leaves a
 * race: the first consumer can be rejected with "this server does not host
 * this topic-partition" while the topic is still being created.
 *
 * Existing topics are left alone, so this is safe to run on every boot.
 */
export async function ensureKafkaTopics({
  brokers,
  clientId,
  topics = KAFKA_TOPICS,
  numPartitions = 3,
}: EnsureKafkaTopicsOptions): Promise<void> {
  const kafka = new Kafka({
    clientId: `${clientId}-admin`,
    brokers,
    retry: { retries: 5, initialRetryTime: 200 },
  });
  const admin = kafka.admin();

  await admin.connect();

  try {
    const existing = new Set(await admin.listTopics());
    const missing = topics.filter((topic) => !existing.has(topic));

    if (missing.length === 0) {
      return;
    }

    await admin.createTopics({
      waitForLeaders: true,
      topics: missing.map((topic) => ({
        topic,
        numPartitions,
        replicationFactor: 1,
      })),
    });
  } finally {
    await admin.disconnect();
  }
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/kafka/order-events-consumer.ts
import { Kafka, type Consumer, type EachMessagePayload } from "kafkajs";
import type { InboxStore } from "../../application/ports/inbox-store.js";
import { CUSTOMER_TOPIC, ORDER_TOPIC } from "./kafka-messages.js";

export type ConsumedEvent = {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  occurredAt: string;
  payload: Record<string, unknown>;
};

export type OrderEventsConsumerOptions = {
  brokers: string[];
  clientId: string;
  groupId: string;
  topics?: string[];
  /** Durable dedupe. Without one, a restart replays recent events. */
  inbox?: InboxStore;
  /** Where a decoded event goes. The default logs one structured line. */
  onEvent?: (event: ConsumedEvent) => Promise<void> | void;
  onError?: (error: unknown) => void;
};

export type OrderEventsConsumer = {
  start(): Promise<void>;
  stop(): Promise<void>;
};

const DEFAULT_TOPICS = [ORDER_TOPIC, CUSTOMER_TOPIC];

/**
 * Delivery is at-least-once, so the same `x-event-id` can arrive twice. The
 * inbox makes that harmless across restarts and across replicas: the insert is
 * the claim, and a duplicate is acknowledged without running the handler.
 *
 * A production projection would record the id in the *same transaction* as the
 * projection update; the port allows that, and the current handler only logs.
 */
export function createOrderEventsConsumer({
  brokers,
  clientId,
  groupId,
  topics = DEFAULT_TOPICS,
  inbox,
  onEvent = (event) => {
    console.log(
      JSON.stringify({
        consumer: "order-events",
        event: event.name,
        id: event.id,
      }),
    );
  },
  onError = (error) => console.error("Kafka consumer error", error),
}: OrderEventsConsumerOptions): OrderEventsConsumer {
  const kafka = new Kafka({ clientId, brokers, retry: { retries: 5 } });
  const consumer: Consumer = kafka.consumer({
    groupId,
    allowAutoTopicCreation: true,
  });

  const handle = async ({ message }: EachMessagePayload): Promise<void> => {
    const headerId = message.headers?.["x-event-id"];
    const raw = message.value?.toString() ?? "";
    let parsed: ConsumedEvent;

    try {
      parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as ConsumedEvent;
    } catch (error) {
      onError(error);
      return;
    }

    const id =
      typeof headerId === "string"
        ? headerId
        : (headerId?.toString() ?? parsed.id);

    if (inbox !== undefined) {
      const firstDelivery = await inbox.markProcessed({
        id,
        eventName: parsed.name,
        processedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
      });

      if (!firstDelivery) {
        return;
      }
    }

    await onEvent({ ...parsed, id });
  };

  return {
    async start() {
      await consumer.connect();
      await consumer.subscribe({ topics, fromBeginning: false });
      await consumer.run({ eachMessage: handle });
    },

    async stop() {
      await consumer.disconnect();
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/observability/metrics.ts
import {
  Counter,
  Histogram,
  Registry,
  collectDefaultMetrics,
} from "prom-client";

export type CheckoutMetricOutcome =
  "completed" | "replayed" | "declined" | "in_progress" | "failed";

export type Metrics = {
  registry: Registry;
  observeHttpRequest(input: {
    method: string;
    route: string;
    statusCode: number;
    durationSeconds: number;
  }): void;
  recordCheckout(outcome: CheckoutMetricOutcome): void;
  recordOutboxDelivery(
    published: number,
    failed: number,
    deadLettered: number,
  ): void;
  render(): Promise<string>;
  contentType: string;
};

/**
 * Metrics are an infrastructure concern: the application returns meaningful
 * outcomes, and this module turns them into series. Cardinality is bounded by
 * using the *route pattern*, never the raw URL.
 */
export function createMetrics(): Metrics {
  const registry = new Registry();
  collectDefaultMetrics({ register: registry });

  const httpRequests = new Counter({
    name: "http_requests_total",
    help: "HTTP requests by method, route, and status",
    labelNames: ["method", "route", "status"] as const,
    registers: [registry],
  });

  const httpDuration = new Histogram({
    name: "http_request_duration_seconds",
    help: "HTTP request duration in seconds",
    labelNames: ["method", "route", "status"] as const,
    buckets: [0.005, 0.01, 0.025, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2.5, 5],
    registers: [registry],
  });

  const checkout = new Counter({
    name: "checkout_total",
    help: "Checkout attempts by outcome",
    labelNames: ["outcome"] as const,
    registers: [registry],
  });

  const outbox = new Counter({
    name: "outbox_messages_total",
    help: "Outbox messages by delivery result",
    labelNames: ["result"] as const,
    registers: [registry],
  });

  return {
    registry,
    contentType: registry.contentType,

    observeHttpRequest({ method, route, statusCode, durationSeconds }) {
      const labels = {
        method,
        route,
        status: String(statusCode),
      };

      httpRequests.inc(labels);
      httpDuration.observe(labels, durationSeconds);
    },

    recordCheckout(outcome) {
      checkout.inc({ outcome });
    },

    recordOutboxDelivery(published, failed, deadLettered) {
      if (published > 0) {
        outbox.inc({ result: "published" }, published);
      }

      if (failed > 0) {
        outbox.inc({ result: "failed" }, failed);
      }

      if (deadLettered > 0) {
        outbox.inc({ result: "dead_lettered" }, deadLettered);
      }
    },

    render() {
      return registry.metrics();
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/outbox/outbox-worker.ts
import type { DispatchOutbox } from "../../application/outbox/dispatch-outbox.types.js";

export type OutboxWorkerOptions = {
  dispatch: DispatchOutbox;
  intervalMs: number;
  onError?: (error: unknown) => void;
};

export type OutboxWorker = {
  start(): void;
  stop(): Promise<void>;
  /** Runs one poll immediately; useful for tests and for shutdown flushing. */
  runOnce(): Promise<void>;
};

/**
 * The scheduling half of the outbox. It knows nothing about events, only when
 * to ask the application to drain a batch. A cron job or a queue-based worker
 * can replace it without touching the dispatcher.
 */
export function createOutboxWorker({
  dispatch,
  intervalMs,
  onError = (error) => console.error("Outbox dispatch failed", error),
}: OutboxWorkerOptions): OutboxWorker {
  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setInterval> | null = null;
  let inFlight: Promise<void> | null = null;
  let stopped = false;

  const poll = async (): Promise<void> => {
    if (inFlight !== null) {
      return inFlight;
    }

    inFlight = (async () => {
      const result = await dispatch();

      if (!result.ok) {
        onError(result.error);
      }
    })()
      .catch(onError)
      .finally(() => {
        inFlight = null;
      });

    return inFlight;
  };

  return {
    start() {
      if (timer !== null || stopped) {
        return;
      }

      timer = setInterval(() => {
        void poll();
      }, intervalMs);
    },

    async runOnce() {
      await poll();
    },

    async stop() {
      stopped = true;

      if (timer !== null) {
        clearInterval(timer);
        timer = null;
      }

      if (inFlight !== null) {
        await inFlight;
      }
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/payments/in-memory-payment-gateway.ts
import type {
  PaymentGateway,
  PaymentGatewayRequest,
} from "../../application/ports/payment-gateway.js";

export type InMemoryPaymentGatewayOptions = {
  /** Decide declines per request; by default every charge succeeds. */
  declineWhen?: (request: PaymentGatewayRequest) => boolean;
  referencePrefix?: string;
};

/**
 * A gateway for local runs and tests: deterministic references, no network,
 * and a decline hook so the failure path is reachable. Stripe (or any real
 * processor) implements the same port.
 *
 * Charges are remembered by idempotency key, mirroring how a real provider
 * protects a retried request from taking money twice.
 */
export function createInMemoryPaymentGateway(
  options: InMemoryPaymentGatewayOptions = {},
): PaymentGateway {
  const prefix = options.referencePrefix ?? "test";
  const references = new Map<string, string>();

  return {
    async charge(request) {
      const previous = references.get(request.idempotencyKey);

      if (previous !== undefined) {
        return { ok: true, value: { providerReference: previous } };
      }

      if (options.declineWhen?.(request) === true) {
        return {
          ok: false,
          error: {
            type: "payment_declined",
            message: "Card was declined by the payment gateway",
          },
        };
      }

      const providerReference = `${prefix}_${references.size + 1}`;
      references.set(request.idempotencyKey, providerReference);

      return {
        ok: true,
        value: { providerReference },
      };
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/in-memory/store.ts
import type { OutboxMessage } from "../../../application/ports/outbox.js";
import type { Account } from "../../../domain/auth/account.js";
import type { Cart, CartId } from "../../../domain/cart/cart.js";
import type { Customer } from "../../../domain/customer/customer.js";
import type { CustomerId } from "../../../domain/customer/customer-id.js";
import type { InventoryItem } from "../../../domain/inventory/inventory-item.js";
import type { Order, OrderId } from "../../../domain/order/order.js";
import type { Product } from "../../../domain/product/product.js";
import type { ProductId } from "../../../domain/product/product-id.js";

/** Every table the in-memory adapters keep, in one swappable object. */
export type InMemoryStore = {
  customers: Map<CustomerId, Customer>;
  accounts: Map<CustomerId, Account>;
  products: Map<ProductId, Product>;
  inventory: Map<ProductId, InventoryItem>;
  carts: Map<CartId, Cart>;
  orders: Map<OrderId, Order>;
  outbox: Map<string, OutboxMessage>;
};

/**
 * Repositories read `store.current` on every operation instead of capturing a
 * Map. That indirection is what lets the transaction adapter swap in a private
 * copy and restore the previous one on rollback.
 */
export type InMemoryStoreRef = {
  current: InMemoryStore;
};

export function createInMemoryStore(): InMemoryStoreRef {
  return {
    current: {
      customers: new Map(),
      accounts: new Map(),
      products: new Map(),
      inventory: new Map(),
      carts: new Map(),
      orders: new Map(),
      outbox: new Map(),
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-account-repository.ts
import type { PrismaClient } from "@prisma/client";
import type { AccountRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/account-repository.js";
import { fromAccountRow, toAccountRow } from "./prisma-mappers.js";
import { currentPrismaClient } from "./prisma-session.js";

export function createPrismaAccountRepository(
  prisma: PrismaClient,
): AccountRepository {
  const db = () => currentPrismaClient() ?? prisma;

  return {
    async save(account) {
      const data = toAccountRow(account);

      await db().account.upsert({
        where: { customerId: account.customerId },
        create: data,
        update: data,
      });
    },

    async findByCustomerId(customerId) {
      const row = await db().account.findUnique({ where: { customerId } });
      return row === null ? null : fromAccountRow(row);
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-client.ts
import { PrismaClient } from "@prisma/client";

export type PrismaConnection = {
  client: PrismaClient;
  check(): Promise<void>;
  close(): Promise<void>;
};

/**
 * The URL is passed explicitly instead of read from the environment here, so
 * the composition root remains the only place that knows configuration.
 */
export async function connectPrisma(url: string): Promise<PrismaConnection> {
  const client = new PrismaClient({
    datasourceUrl: url,
    log: ["warn", "error"],
  });

  await client.$connect();

  return {
    client,
    async check() {
      await client.$queryRaw`SELECT 1`;
    },
    async close() {
      await client.$disconnect();
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-customer-repository.ts
import type { PrismaClient } from "@prisma/client";
import type { CustomerRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/customer-repository.js";
import { fromCustomerRow, toCustomerRow } from "./prisma-mappers.js";
import { currentPrismaClient } from "./prisma-session.js";

export function createPrismaCustomerRepository(
  prisma: PrismaClient,
): CustomerRepository {
  const db = () => currentPrismaClient() ?? prisma;

  return {
    async save(customer) {
      const data = toCustomerRow(customer);

      await db().customer.upsert({
        where: { id: customer.id },
        create: data,
        update: data,
      });
    },

    async findById(id) {
      const row = await db().customer.findUnique({ where: { id } });
      return row === null ? null : fromCustomerRow(row);
    },

    async findByEmail(email) {
      const row = await db().customer.findUnique({
        where: { email: email.trim().toLowerCase() },
      });
      return row === null ? null : fromCustomerRow(row);
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-dependencies.ts
import type { InboxStore } from "../../../application/ports/inbox-store.js";
import type { OutboxRepository } from "../../../application/ports/outbox.js";
import type { AccountRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/account-repository.js";
import type { CartRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/cart-repository.js";
import type { CustomerRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/customer-repository.js";
import type { InventoryRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/inventory-repository.js";
import type { OrderRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/order-repository.js";
import type { ProductRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/product-repository.js";
import type { UnitOfWork } from "../../../application/ports/unit-of-work.js";
import { createPrismaUnitOfWork } from "../../transactions/prisma-unit-of-work.js";
import { createPrismaAccountRepository } from "./prisma-account-repository.js";
import { connectPrisma } from "./prisma-client.js";
import { createPrismaCartRepository } from "./prisma-cart-repository.js";
import { createPrismaCustomerRepository } from "./prisma-customer-repository.js";
import { createPrismaInboxStore } from "./prisma-inbox-store.js";
import { createPrismaInventoryRepository } from "./prisma-inventory-repository.js";
import { createPrismaOrderRepository } from "./prisma-order-repository.js";
import { createPrismaOutboxRepository } from "./prisma-outbox-repository.js";
import { createPrismaProductRepository } from "./prisma-product-repository.js";

export type PersistenceDependencies = {
  customers: CustomerRepository;
  accounts: AccountRepository;
  products: ProductRepository;
  inventory: InventoryRepository;
  carts: CartRepository;
  orders: OrderRepository;
  outbox: OutboxRepository;
  inbox: InboxStore;
  unitOfWork: UnitOfWork;
  /** Readiness probe: fails when the database is unreachable. */
  check(): Promise<void>;
  close(): Promise<void>;
};

export type PrismaPersistenceOptions = {
  url: string;
};

/**
 * Builds the PostgreSQL adapter set. Migrations are applied by `npm run
 * db:migrate` (and by the container entrypoint), never by this module.
 */
export async function buildPrismaPersistence({
  url,
}: PrismaPersistenceOptions): Promise<PersistenceDependencies> {
  const connection = await connectPrisma(url);

  return {
    customers: createPrismaCustomerRepository(connection.client),
    accounts: createPrismaAccountRepository(connection.client),
    products: createPrismaProductRepository(connection.client),
    inventory: createPrismaInventoryRepository(connection.client),
    carts: createPrismaCartRepository(connection.client),
    orders: createPrismaOrderRepository(connection.client),
    outbox: createPrismaOutboxRepository(connection.client),
    inbox: createPrismaInboxStore(connection.client),
    unitOfWork: createPrismaUnitOfWork(connection.client),
    check: connection.check,
    close: connection.close,
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-inbox-store.ts
import type { PrismaClient } from "@prisma/client";
import type { InboxStore } from "../../../application/ports/inbox-store.js";

/**
 * `createMany` with `skipDuplicates` becomes `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO
 * NOTHING`: the unique primary key decides the winner, the row count says
 * whether this caller is the first delivery, and a duplicate is not an
 * exception to log. No read-then-write window.
 */
export function createPrismaInboxStore(prisma: PrismaClient): InboxStore {
  return {
    async markProcessed({ id, eventName, processedAt }) {
      const { count } = await prisma.processedEvent.createMany({
        data: [{ id, eventName, processedAt: new Date(processedAt) }],
        skipDuplicates: true,
      });

      return count === 1;
    },

    async hasProcessed(id) {
      const row = await prisma.processedEvent.findUnique({ where: { id } });
      return row !== null;
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-inventory-repository.ts
import type { PrismaClient } from "@prisma/client";
import type { InventoryRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/inventory-repository.js";
import { fromInventoryRow, toInventoryRow } from "./prisma-mappers.js";
import { currentPrismaClient } from "./prisma-session.js";

export function createPrismaInventoryRepository(
  prisma: PrismaClient,
): InventoryRepository {
  const db = () => currentPrismaClient() ?? prisma;

  return {
    async save(item) {
      const data = toInventoryRow(item);

      // The unique index on productId is the storage guarantee: one stock
      // record per product, whichever id the aggregate happens to carry.
      await db().inventoryItem.upsert({
        where: { productId: item.productId },
        create: data,
        update: data,
      });
    },

    async findByProductId(productId) {
      const row = await db().inventoryItem.findUnique({
        where: { productId },
      });
      return row === null ? null : fromInventoryRow(row);
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-mappers.ts
import type { Prisma } from "@prisma/client";
import type { OutboxMessage } from "../../../application/ports/outbox.js";
import type { Account } from "../../../domain/auth/account.js";
import { isRole } from "../../../domain/auth/role.js";
import type { Cart as CartAggregate } from "../../../domain/cart/cart.js";
import type { CartItem } from "../../../domain/cart/cart-item.js";
import type { Customer } from "../../../domain/customer/customer.js";
import type { InventoryItem } from "../../../domain/inventory/inventory-item.js";
import type { Order } from "../../../domain/order/order.js";
import type { OrderItem } from "../../../domain/order/order-item.js";
import type { OrderStatus } from "../../../domain/order/order-status.js";
import type { Product } from "../../../domain/product/product.js";

/**
 * The database stores timestamps as `timestamptz` and line items as JSON
 * snapshots. The domain speaks ISO strings and typed arrays. This module is the
 * only place that translates, so neither side borrows the other's shape.
 */
export function toIso(value: Date): string {
  return value.toISOString();
}

export function toDate(value: string): Date {
  return new Date(value);
}

export type CustomerRow = {
  id: string;
  email: string;
  name: string;
  createdAt: Date;
};

export function fromCustomerRow(row: CustomerRow): Customer {
  return {
    id: row.id,
    email: row.email,
    name: row.name,
    createdAt: toIso(row.createdAt),
  };
}

export function toCustomerRow(customer: Customer) {
  return {
    id: customer.id,
    email: customer.email,
    name: customer.name,
    createdAt: toDate(customer.createdAt),
  };
}

export type AccountRow = {
  customerId: string;
  passwordHash: string;
  role: string;
  createdAt: Date;
  updatedAt: Date;
};

export function fromAccountRow(row: AccountRow): Account {
  return {
    customerId: row.customerId,
    passwordHash: row.passwordHash,
    // An unreadable role is downgraded, never guessed upward: a corrupted row
    // must not become an administrator.
    role: isRole(row.role) ? row.role : "customer",
    createdAt: toIso(row.createdAt),
    updatedAt: toIso(row.updatedAt),
  };
}

export function toAccountRow(account: Account) {
  return {
    customerId: account.customerId,
    passwordHash: account.passwordHash,
    role: account.role,
    createdAt: toDate(account.createdAt),
    updatedAt: toDate(account.updatedAt),
  };
}

export type ProductRow = {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  amountInMinorUnits: number;
  currency: string;
  createdAt: Date;
};

export function fromProductRow(row: ProductRow): Product {
  return {
    id: row.id,
    name: row.name,
    price: {
      amountInMinorUnits: row.amountInMinorUnits,
      currency: row.currency,
    },
    createdAt: toIso(row.createdAt),
  };
}

export function toProductRow(product: Product) {
  return {
    id: product.id,
    name: product.name,
    amountInMinorUnits: product.price.amountInMinorUnits,
    currency: product.price.currency,
    createdAt: toDate(product.createdAt),
  };
}

export type InventoryRow = {
  id: string;
  productId: string;
  available: number;
  reserved: number;
  updatedAt: Date;
};

export function fromInventoryRow(row: InventoryRow): InventoryItem {
  return {
    id: row.id,
    productId: row.productId,
    available: row.available,
    reserved: row.reserved,
    updatedAt: toIso(row.updatedAt),
  };
}

export function toInventoryRow(item: InventoryItem) {
  return {
    id: item.id,
    productId: item.productId,
    available: item.available,
    reserved: item.reserved,
    updatedAt: toDate(item.updatedAt),
  };
}

export type CartRow = {
  id: string;
  customerId: string;
  items: Prisma.JsonValue;
  createdAt: Date;
  updatedAt: Date;
};

export function fromCartRow(row: CartRow): CartAggregate {
  return {
    id: row.id,
    customerId: row.customerId,
    items: row.items as unknown as CartItem[],
    createdAt: toIso(row.createdAt),
    updatedAt: toIso(row.updatedAt),
  };
}

export function toCartRow(cart: CartAggregate) {
  return {
    id: cart.id,
    customerId: cart.customerId,
    items: cart.items as unknown as Prisma.InputJsonValue,
    createdAt: toDate(cart.createdAt),
    updatedAt: toDate(cart.updatedAt),
  };
}

export type OrderRow = {
  id: string;
  customerId: string;
  items: Prisma.JsonValue;
  totalAmountInMinor: number;
  currency: string;
  status: string;
  createdAt: Date;
  updatedAt: Date;
  paidAt: Date | null;
  cancelledAt: Date | null;
};

export function fromOrderRow(row: OrderRow): Order {
  return {
    id: row.id,
    customerId: row.customerId,
    items: row.items as unknown as OrderItem[],
    total: {
      amountInMinorUnits: row.totalAmountInMinor,
      currency: row.currency,
    },
    status: row.status as OrderStatus,
    createdAt: toIso(row.createdAt),
    updatedAt: toIso(row.updatedAt),
    paidAt: row.paidAt === null ? null : toIso(row.paidAt),
    cancelledAt: row.cancelledAt === null ? null : toIso(row.cancelledAt),
  };
}

export function toOrderRow(order: Order) {
  return {
    id: order.id,
    customerId: order.customerId,
    items: order.items as unknown as Prisma.InputJsonValue,
    totalAmountInMinor: order.total.amountInMinorUnits,
    currency: order.total.currency,
    status: order.status,
    createdAt: toDate(order.createdAt),
    updatedAt: toDate(order.updatedAt),
    paidAt: order.paidAt === null ? null : toDate(order.paidAt),
    cancelledAt: order.cancelledAt === null ? null : toDate(order.cancelledAt),
  };
}

export type OutboxRow = {
  id: string;
  eventName: string;
  occurredAt: Date;
  payload: Prisma.JsonValue;
  createdAt: Date;
  publishedAt: Date | null;
  attempts: number;
  lastError: string | null;
  nextAttemptAt: Date;
  deadLetteredAt: Date | null;
};

export function fromOutboxRow(row: OutboxRow): OutboxMessage {
  return {
    id: row.id,
    event: {
      name: row.eventName,
      occurredAt: toIso(row.occurredAt),
      payload: row.payload as Record<string, unknown>,
    },
    createdAt: toIso(row.createdAt),
    publishedAt: row.publishedAt === null ? null : toIso(row.publishedAt),
    attempts: row.attempts,
    lastError: row.lastError,
    nextAttemptAt: toIso(row.nextAttemptAt),
    deadLetteredAt:
      row.deadLetteredAt === null ? null : toIso(row.deadLetteredAt),
  };
}

export function toOutboxRow(message: OutboxMessage) {
  return {
    id: message.id,
    eventName: message.event.name,
    occurredAt: toDate(message.event.occurredAt),
    payload: message.event.payload as unknown as Prisma.InputJsonValue,
    createdAt: toDate(message.createdAt),
    publishedAt:
      message.publishedAt === null ? null : toDate(message.publishedAt),
    attempts: message.attempts,
    lastError: message.lastError,
    nextAttemptAt: toDate(message.nextAttemptAt),
    deadLetteredAt:
      message.deadLetteredAt === null ? null : toDate(message.deadLetteredAt),
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-order-repository.ts
import type { PrismaClient } from "@prisma/client";
import type { OrderRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/order-repository.js";
import { fromOrderRow, toOrderRow } from "./prisma-mappers.js";
import { currentPrismaClient } from "./prisma-session.js";

export function createPrismaOrderRepository(
  prisma: PrismaClient,
): OrderRepository {
  const db = () => currentPrismaClient() ?? prisma;

  return {
    async save(order) {
      const data = toOrderRow(order);

      await db().order.upsert({
        where: { id: order.id },
        create: data,
        update: data,
      });
    },

    async findById(id) {
      const row = await db().order.findUnique({ where: { id } });
      return row === null ? null : fromOrderRow(row);
    },

    async findByCustomerId(customerId) {
      const rows = await db().order.findMany({
        where: { customerId },
        orderBy: [{ createdAt: "asc" }, { id: "asc" }],
      });

      return rows.map(fromOrderRow);
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-outbox-repository.ts
import type { PrismaClient } from "@prisma/client";
import type { OutboxRepository } from "../../../application/ports/outbox.js";
import { fromOutboxRow, toDate, toOutboxRow } from "./prisma-mappers.js";
import { currentPrismaClient } from "./prisma-session.js";

/**
 * Delivery scheduling is a query: unpublished, not dead-lettered, and past
 * `nextAttemptAt`. That keeps the backoff in the database, so a restart does
 * not reset it and a second worker sees the same truth.
 */
export function createPrismaOutboxRepository(
  prisma: PrismaClient,
): OutboxRepository {
  const db = () => currentPrismaClient() ?? prisma;

  return {
    async add(message) {
      await db().outboxMessage.create({ data: toOutboxRow(message) });
    },

    async findPending(limit, now) {
      const rows = await db().outboxMessage.findMany({
        where: {
          publishedAt: null,
          deadLetteredAt: null,
          nextAttemptAt: { lte: toDate(now) },
        },
        orderBy: [{ nextAttemptAt: "asc" }, { createdAt: "asc" }],
        take: limit,
      });

      return rows.map(fromOutboxRow);
    },

    async markPublished(id, publishedAt) {
      await db().outboxMessage.update({
        where: { id },
        data: { publishedAt: toDate(publishedAt), lastError: null },
      });
    },

    async markFailed(id, failure) {
      await db().outboxMessage.update({
        where: { id },
        data: {
          attempts: { increment: 1 },
          lastError: failure.error,
          nextAttemptAt: toDate(failure.nextAttemptAt),
          deadLetteredAt:
            failure.deadLetteredAt === null
              ? null
              : toDate(failure.deadLetteredAt),
        },
      });
    },

    async findDeadLettered(limit) {
      const rows = await db().outboxMessage.findMany({
        where: { deadLetteredAt: { not: null } },
        orderBy: { deadLetteredAt: "asc" },
        take: limit,
      });

      return rows.map(fromOutboxRow);
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-product-repository.ts
import type { PrismaClient } from "@prisma/client";

export function createPrismaProductRepository(
  prisma: PrismaClient,
): ProductRepository {
  const db = () => currentPrismaClient() ?? prisma;

  return {
    async save(product) {
      const data = toProductRow(product);

      await db().product.upsert({

        create: data,

      return rows.map(fromProductRow);

}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/prisma-session.ts
import { AsyncLocalStorage } from "node:async_hooks";
import type { Prisma } from "@prisma/client";

/**
 * Repositories need the transaction-scoped client to join a unit of work.
 * Threading it through every port would leak persistence into the
 * application, so it travels through async context instead, invisible to use
 * cases and identical in shape to the previous document-store adapter.
 */
const storage = new AsyncLocalStorage<Prisma.TransactionClient>();

export function runWithPrismaClient<T>(
  client: Prisma.TransactionClient,
  work: () => Promise<T>,
): Promise<T> {
  return storage.run(client, work);
}

export function currentPrismaClient(): Prisma.TransactionClient | undefined {
  return storage.getStore();
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/repositories/in-memory-account-repository.ts
import type { AccountRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/account-repository.js";
import type { InMemoryStoreRef } from "../in-memory/store.js";

/** Copies on read and write so callers can never mutate stored state. */
export function createInMemoryAccountRepository(
  store: InMemoryStoreRef,
): AccountRepository {
  return {
    async save(account) {
      store.current.accounts.set(account.customerId, { ...account });
    },

    async findByCustomerId(customerId) {
      const account = store.current.accounts.get(customerId);
      return account ? { ...account } : null;
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/repositories/in-memory-cart-repository.ts
import type { CartRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/cart-repository.js";
import type { Cart } from "../../../domain/cart/cart.js";
import type { InMemoryStoreRef } from "../in-memory/store.js";

function clone(cart: Cart): Cart {
  return {
    ...cart,
    items: cart.items.map((item) => ({
      ...item,
      unitPrice: { ...item.unitPrice },
    })),
  };
}

export function createInMemoryCartRepository(
  store: InMemoryStoreRef,
): CartRepository {
  return {
    async save(cart) {
      store.current.carts.set(cart.id, clone(cart));
    },

    async findById(id) {
      const cart = store.current.carts.get(id);
      return cart ? clone(cart) : null;
    },

    async findByCustomerId(customerId) {
      for (const cart of store.current.carts.values()) {
        if (cart.customerId === customerId) {
          return clone(cart);
        }
      }

      return null;
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/repositories/in-memory-customer-repository.ts
import type { CustomerRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/customer-repository.js";
import type { InMemoryStoreRef } from "../in-memory/store.js";

/** Copies on read and write so callers can never mutate stored state. */
export function createInMemoryCustomerRepository(
  store: InMemoryStoreRef,
): CustomerRepository {
  return {
    async save(customer) {
      store.current.customers.set(customer.id, { ...customer });
    },

    async findById(id) {
      const customer = store.current.customers.get(id);
      return customer ? { ...customer } : null;
    },

    async findByEmail(email) {
      const normalized = email.trim().toLowerCase();

      for (const customer of store.current.customers.values()) {
        if (customer.email === normalized) {
          return { ...customer };
        }
      }

      return null;
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/repositories/in-memory-order-repository.ts
import type { OrderRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/order-repository.js";
import type { Order } from "../../../domain/order/order.js";
import type { InMemoryStoreRef } from "../in-memory/store.js";

function clone(order: Order): Order {
  return {
    ...order,
    items: order.items.map((item) => ({
      ...item,
      unitPrice: { ...item.unitPrice },
      lineTotal: { ...item.lineTotal },
    })),
    total: { ...order.total },
  };
}

/** Orders are append-only in practice; saving replaces the stored snapshot. */
export function createInMemoryOrderRepository(
  store: InMemoryStoreRef,
): OrderRepository {
  return {
    async save(order) {
      store.current.orders.set(order.id, clone(order));
    },

    async findById(id) {
      const order = store.current.orders.get(id);
      return order ? clone(order) : null;
    },

    async findByCustomerId(customerId) {
      return [...store.current.orders.values()]
        .filter((order) => order.customerId === customerId)
        .map(clone);
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/repositories/in-memory-outbox-repository.ts
import type { OutboxRepository } from "../../../application/ports/outbox.js";
import type { InMemoryStoreRef } from "../in-memory/store.js";

export function createInMemoryOutboxRepository(
  store: InMemoryStoreRef,
): OutboxRepository {
  return {
    async add(message) {
      store.current.outbox.set(message.id, structuredClone(message));
    },

    async findPending(limit, now) {
      return [...store.current.outbox.values()]
        .filter(
          (message) =>
            message.publishedAt === null &&
            message.deadLetteredAt === null &&
            message.nextAttemptAt <= now,
        )
        .sort((left, right) =>
          left.nextAttemptAt.localeCompare(right.nextAttemptAt),
        )
        .slice(0, limit)
        .map((message) => structuredClone(message));
    },

    async markPublished(id, publishedAt) {
      const message = store.current.outbox.get(id);

      if (message === undefined) {
        return;
      }

      store.current.outbox.set(id, {
        ...structuredClone(message),
        publishedAt,
        lastError: null,
      });
    },

    async markFailed(id, failure) {
      const message = store.current.outbox.get(id);

      if (message === undefined) {
        return;
      }

      store.current.outbox.set(id, {
        ...structuredClone(message),
        attempts: message.attempts + 1,
        lastError: failure.error,
        nextAttemptAt: failure.nextAttemptAt,
        deadLetteredAt: failure.deadLetteredAt,
      });
    },

    async findDeadLettered(limit) {
      return [...store.current.outbox.values()]
        .filter((message) => message.deadLetteredAt !== null)
        .sort((left, right) =>
          (left.deadLetteredAt ?? "").localeCompare(right.deadLetteredAt ?? ""),
        )
        .slice(0, limit)
        .map((message) => structuredClone(message));
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/persistence/repositories/in-memory-product-repository.ts
import type { ProductRepository } from "../../../application/ports/repositories/product-repository.js";
import type { InMemoryStoreRef } from "../in-memory/store.js";

export function createInMemoryProductRepository(

      store.current.products.set(product.id, { ...product });
    },

    async findById(id) {
      const product = store.current.products.get(id);
      return product ? { ...product } : null;
    },

    async findAll() {

        ...product,
      }));
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/rate-limit/in-memory-rate-limiter.ts
import type {
  RateLimitDecision,
  RateLimiter,
} from "../../application/ports/rate-limiter.js";

export type InMemoryRateLimiterOptions = {
  limit: number;
  windowSeconds: number;
  /** Injectable so tests can move time without sleeping. */
  now?: () => number;
};

type Window = {
  count: number;
  resetAt: number;
};

/**
 * A per-process fixed window: correct for one instance, and the fallback when
 * Redis is not configured. With several instances it undercounts, which is why
 * the Redis adapter exists.
 */
export function createInMemoryRateLimiter({
  limit,
  windowSeconds,
  now = Date.now,
}: InMemoryRateLimiterOptions): RateLimiter {
  const windows = new Map<string, Window>();
  const windowMs = windowSeconds * 1000;

  return {
    async consume(key): Promise<RateLimitDecision> {
      const timestamp = now();
      const existing = windows.get(key);

      if (existing === undefined || existing.resetAt <= timestamp) {
        windows.set(key, { count: 1, resetAt: timestamp + windowMs });

        return {
          allowed: true,
          limit,
          remaining: Math.max(0, limit - 1),
          resetInSeconds: windowSeconds,
        };
      }

      existing.count += 1;

      return {
        allowed: existing.count <= limit,
        limit,
        remaining: Math.max(0, limit - existing.count),
        resetInSeconds: Math.max(
          1,
          Math.ceil((existing.resetAt - timestamp) / 1000),
        ),
      };
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/redis/redis-cache.ts
import type { Redis } from "ioredis";
import type { Cache } from "../../application/ports/cache.js";

/** JSON is the wire format; keys are namespaced so Redis stays inspectable. */
export function createRedisCache(client: Redis, prefix = "cache:"): Cache {
  return {
    async get<T>(key: string): Promise<T | null> {
      const raw = await client.get(`${prefix}${key}`);

      if (raw === null) {
        return null;
      }

      try {
        return JSON.parse(raw) as T;
      } catch {
        // A corrupt entry is a cache miss, never a request failure.
        await client.del(`${prefix}${key}`);
        return null;
      }
    },

    async set<T>(key: string, value: T, ttlSeconds: number): Promise<void> {
      await client.set(
        `${prefix}${key}`,
        JSON.stringify(value),
        "EX",
        ttlSeconds,
      );
    },

    async delete(key: string): Promise<void> {
      await client.del(`${prefix}${key}`);
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/redis/redis-client.ts
import { Redis } from "ioredis";

export type RedisConnection = {
  client: Redis;
  close(): Promise<void>;
  check(): Promise<void>;
};

/**
 * One connection shared by the cache, idempotency, and rate limiting adapters.
 * `lazyConnect` keeps start-up honest: the composition root decides when to
 * connect, and a failed connection surfaces at that point instead of later.
 */
export async function connectRedis(url: string): Promise<RedisConnection> {
  const client = new Redis(url, {
    lazyConnect: true,
    maxRetriesPerRequest: 3,
    enableOfflineQueue: true,
    connectTimeout: 5_000,
  });

  client.on("error", (error) => {
    console.error("Redis connection error", error.message);
  });

  await client.connect();

  return {
    client,
    async close() {
      await client.quit();
    },
    async check() {
      await client.ping();
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/redis/redis-idempotency-store.ts
import type { Redis } from "ioredis";
import type {
  IdempotencyRecord,
  IdempotencyReservation,
  IdempotencyStore,
} from "../../application/ports/idempotency-store.js";

const RECORD_PREFIX = "idempotency:record:";
const CLAIM_PREFIX = "idempotency:claim:";

/**
 * `SET NX EX` is the whole trick: the first request to claim a key wins, and
 * every concurrent retry gets `busy` instead of running the checkout again.
 *
 * The claim and the record are separate keys on purpose. Saving the record
 * removes the claim, so a *completed* retry replays, while an *in-flight*
 * retry is refused with 409 rather than double-charging.
 */
export function createRedisIdempotencyStore(client: Redis): IdempotencyStore {
  return {
    async find<TResult>(key: string) {
      const raw = await client.get(`${RECORD_PREFIX}${key}`);

      if (raw === null) {
        return null;
      }

      try {
        return JSON.parse(raw) as IdempotencyRecord<TResult>;
      } catch {
        return null;
      }
    },

    async save<TResult>(
      key: string,
      record: IdempotencyRecord<TResult>,
      ttlSeconds: number,
    ) {
      await client
        .multi()
        .set(`${RECORD_PREFIX}${key}`, JSON.stringify(record), "EX", ttlSeconds)
        .del(`${CLAIM_PREFIX}${key}`)
        .exec();
    },

    async acquire(
      key: string,
      ttlSeconds: number,
    ): Promise<IdempotencyReservation> {
      const result = await client.set(
        `${CLAIM_PREFIX}${key}`,
        "1",
        "EX",
        ttlSeconds,
        "NX",
      );

      return result === "OK" ? "acquired" : "busy";
    },

    async release(key: string) {
      await client.del(`${CLAIM_PREFIX}${key}`);
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/redis/redis-rate-limiter.ts
import type { Redis } from "ioredis";
import type {
  RateLimitDecision,
  RateLimiter,
} from "../../application/ports/rate-limiter.js";

/**
 * INCR plus EXPIRE must be one step, otherwise a crash between them leaves a
 * counter with no window and the caller is limited forever. A short Lua script
 * runs atomically inside Redis.
 */
const LUA = `
local count = redis.call('INCR', KEYS[1])
if count == 1 then
  redis.call('EXPIRE', KEYS[1], ARGV[1])
end
local ttl = redis.call('TTL', KEYS[1])
return { count, ttl }
`;

export type RedisRateLimiterOptions = {
  limit: number;
  windowSeconds: number;
  prefix?: string;
};

export function createRedisRateLimiter(
  client: Redis,
  { limit, windowSeconds, prefix = "ratelimit:" }: RedisRateLimiterOptions,
): RateLimiter {
  return {
    async consume(key): Promise<RateLimitDecision> {
      const response = (await client.eval(
        LUA,
        1,
        `${prefix}${key}`,
        windowSeconds,
      )) as [number, number];

      const count = response[0] ?? 0;
      const ttl = response[1] ?? windowSeconds;

      return {
        allowed: count <= limit,
        limit,
        remaining: Math.max(0, limit - count),
        resetInSeconds: ttl > 0 ? ttl : windowSeconds,
      };
    },
  };
}
````

````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/transactions/in-memory-unit-of-work.ts
import type {
  UnitOfWork,
  UnitOfWorkError,
} from "../../application/ports/unit-of-work.js";
import type { Result } from "../../domain/shared/result.js";
import type { InMemoryStoreRef } from "../persistence/in-memory/store.js";

/**
 * A real transaction boundary over in-memory maps, not a try/catch facade:
 *
 *   1. snapshot the whole store
 *   2. let the work write to a private copy
 *   3. keep the copy only when the work reports success
 *   4. restore the snapshot on failure or on a thrown error
 *
 * Transactions are serialised through a promise queue so two of them cannot
 * both claim the same stock. A PostgreSQL implementation gets this for free
 * from the database; the application code cannot tell which one it has.
 */
export function createInMemoryUnitOfWork(store: InMemoryStoreRef): UnitOfWork {
  let tail: Promise<unknown> = Promise.resolve();

  return {
    execute<T, E>(
      work: () => Promise<Result<T, E>>,
    ): Promise<Result<T, E | UnitOfWorkError>> {
      const run = async (): Promise<Result<T, E | UnitOfWorkError>> => {
        const snapshot = structuredClone(store.current);

        try {
          const result = await work();

          if (!result.ok) {
            store.current = snapshot;
          }

          return result;
        } catch (error) {
          store.current = snapshot;

          return {
            ok: false,
            error: {
              type: "transaction_failed",
              message:
                error instanceof Error
                  ? error.message
                  : "The transaction could not be completed",
            },
          };
        }
      };

      const settled = tail.then(run, run);
      tail = settled.then(
        () => undefined,
        () => undefined,
      );

      return settled;
    },
  };
}

````
````ecommerce-backend/src/infrastructure/transactions/prisma-unit-of-work.ts
import { Prisma, type PrismaClient } from "@prisma/client";
import type {
  UnitOfWork,
  UnitOfWorkError,
} from "../../application/ports/unit-of-work.js";
import type { Result } from "../../domain/shared/result.js";
import { runWithPrismaClient } from "../persistence/prisma/prisma-session.js";

const MAX_ATTEMPTS = 3;

/** P2034 is Prisma's write-conflict / deadlock code: retry the transaction. */
const WRITE_CONFLICT = "P2034";

/** Thrown inside `$transaction` to turn a business failure into a rollback. */
class RollbackSignal extends Error {
  readonly result: Result<unknown, unknown>;

  constructor(result: Result<unknown, unknown>) {
    super("transaction rolled back");
    this.result = result;
  }
}

function messageOf(error: unknown): string {
  return error instanceof Error ? error.message : "Transaction failed";
}

function isWriteConflict(error: unknown): boolean {
  return (
    error instanceof Prisma.PrismaClientKnownRequestError &&
    error.code === WRITE_CONFLICT
  );
}

/**
 * A real PostgreSQL transaction. The work runs inside `$transaction`; a
 * business failure is signalled by throwing a sentinel, because returning a
 * failure value would commit the partial work. A write conflict (two checkouts
 * claiming the same stock) is retried against fresh data.
 *
 * Serializable isolation is deliberate: the read-check-write in
 * `create-order` must not interleave with another reservation.
 */
export function createPrismaUnitOfWork(prisma: PrismaClient): UnitOfWork {
  return {
    async execute<T, E>(
      work: () => Promise<Result<T, E>>,
    ): Promise<Result<T, E | UnitOfWorkError>> {
      let lastError: unknown = null;

      for (let attempt = 1; attempt <= MAX_ATTEMPTS; attempt += 1) {
        try {
          return await prisma.$transaction(
            async (tx) => {
              const result = await runWithPrismaClient(tx, work);

              if (!result.ok) {
                throw new RollbackSignal(result);
              }

              return result;
            },
            {
              isolationLevel: Prisma.TransactionIsolationLevel.Serializable,
              maxWait: 5_000,
              timeout: 15_000,
            },
          );
        } catch (error) {
          if (error instanceof RollbackSignal) {
            return error.result as Result<T, E | UnitOfWorkError>;
          }

          lastError = error;

          if (isWriteConflict(error) && attempt < MAX_ATTEMPTS) {
            continue;
          }

          return {
            ok: false,
            error: { type: "transaction_failed", message: messageOf(error) },
          };
        }
      }

      return {
        ok: false,
        error: { type: "transaction_failed", message: messageOf(lastError) },
      };
    },
  };
}
````
